Notes 
 
1. The General Circular Nos. 14/2020 dated April 08, 2020, 17/2020 dated April 13, 2020, and various 
subsequent Circulars latest being 03/2025 dated September 22, 2025 issued by the Ministry of Corporate 
Affairs (collectively referred to as “MCA Circulars”) permitted the holding of the Extra-Ordinary General 
Meeting (“ EGM/ Meeting ”) through Video Conferencing/Other Audio Visual Means (“ VC/ OAVM ”), 
without the physical presence of the Members at a common venue. In compliance with the provisions of 
the Companies Act, 2013 (the “ Act”), Securities and Exchange Board of India (“ SEBI”) (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“ SEBI LODR Regulations ”) and the MCA Circulars, the 
Meeting of the Company is being held through VC/OAVM. The venue of the Meeting shall be deemed to be 
the Registered Office of the Company. 
 
2. A statement pursuant to Section 102(1) of the Act (Explanatory Statement), setting out the material facts 
in respect of Special Businesses proposed to be transacted at the Meeting along with additional information 
is annexed herewith.  
 
3. A Member entitled to attend and vote at the Meeting is entitled to appoint one or more proxies to attend 
and vote on its behalf and the proxy need not be a Member of the Company. However, pursuant to MCA 
Circulars, the Meeting will be held through VC/OAVM and the physical attendance of Members in any case 
has been dispensed with. Accordingly, the facility for appointment of proxies by the Members will not be 
available for the Meeting and hence the Proxy Form, Attendance Slip and Route Map are not annexed to 
this Notice.  
 
4. Pursuant to the provisions of Section 113 of the Act, Corporate Members are entitled to appoint authorized 
representatives to attend and vote on their behalf at the Meeting, including for remote e-voting and voting 
during the Meeting conducted through VC/OAVM.  
 
Corporate Members intending to a Ʃend the Mee Ɵng through their authorized representa Ɵves are 
requested to send a Cer Ɵﬁed True Copy of the Board Resolu Ɵon and if applicable, a Power of A Ʃorney, 
authorizing such representa Ɵve to aƩend and vote at the  MeeƟng. The said documents should be submi Ʃed 
in PDF or JPEG format from their registered e-mail address to shareholders@iiﬂ.com .   
 
Institutional Investors are encouraged to attend and vote at the Meeting.  
 
5. In compliance with the aforesaid MCA Circulars and SEBI LODR Regulations, Notice of the Meeting is being 
sent only through electronic mode to those Members whose email addresses are registered with the 
Company/ Depositories as on Friday, November 06, 2026. Members may note that the Notice of the 
Meeting will also be available on the website of the Company i.e. www.iifl.com , and the website of the 
Stock Exchanges i.e. BSE Limited and National Stock Exchange of India Limited at www.bseindia.com  and 
www.nseindia.com , respectively. The Notice of the Meeting is also available on the website of MUFG Intime 
India Private Limited (Formerly known as Link Intime India Private Limited) (“MUFG Intime”), Registrar to 
an Issue and Share Transfer Agent (“RTA”) of the Company at https://instavote.linkintime.co.in .

Company is pleased to provide its Members with the facility of voting by electronic means. The instructions 
and other information relating to remote e-voting are provided in Note No. 16 of this Notice.

quorum under Section 103 of the Act.

13. SEBI, vide its Circular No. SEBI/HO/MIRSD/MIRSD_ RTAMB/P/CIR/2022/8 dated January 25, 2022, 
mandated Listed Companies to issue securities in demat form only while processing service requests such 
as the issue of duplicate securities certificates, claims from unclaimed suspense accounts, renewal/ 
exchange of securities certificates, endorsement, subdivision/splitting of securities certificates, 
consolidation of securities certificates/folios, transmission, and transposition. Furthermore, SEBI, through 
Circular No. SEBI/HO/MIRSD/MIRSD_RTAMB/P/CIR/2022/65 dated May 18, 2022, simplified the procedure 
and standardized the format of documents for the transmission of securities. Accordingly, Members are 
requested to submit service requests by duly filling and signing Form ISR-4 or Form ISR-5 (for transmission). 
The formats of these forms are available on the Company’s website at 
https://www.iifl.com/finance/investor-information and on the website of MUFG Intime. 
 
Members holding equity shares of the Company in physical form are requested to get their equity shares 
converted into demat/electronic form to avail the inherent benefits of dematerialization. Please note that, 
as per SEBI Regulations, physical transfer of equity shares and issuance of equity shares in physical form 
has been disallowed. 
 
Members are accordingly requested to get in touch with any DP having registration with SEBI to open a 
Demat account or alternatively, contact MUFG Intime to seek guidance in relation to the demat procedure. 
Members may also visit website of depositories viz. National Securities Depository Limited at 
https://nsdl.co.in/faqs/faq.php or Central Depository Services (India) Limited at 
https://www.cdslindia.com/Investors/faqs.html for further understanding the demat procedure.

in force), and the provisions of Chapter V of the SEBI ICDR Regula Ɵons, any preferen Ɵal issue of securi Ɵes 
by way of private placement is required to be approved by the Members of the Company by way of a 
Special Resolu Ɵon. 
 
Therefore, the consent of the Members of the Company is being sought by a Special Resolu Ɵon to enable 
the Board to issue the Subscrip Ɵon Shares to Investor 1, Subscrip Ɵon Warrants to the respec Ɵve 
Promoters and the Anchorage SubscripƟon Shares  to the respec Ɵve Anchorage Investors in accordance 
with the provisions of the Act, SEBI ICDR Regula Ɵons and other applicable laws, as per the details 
menƟoned at Item No. 2 of the No Ɵce. 
  
Background and Purpose of the Preferen Ɵal Issue 
  
The Company is one of India’s leading retail-focused non-banking ﬁnancial companies (“ NBFCs”), with a 
pan-India network of 5,091 branches and a granular secured retail loan book. The Company has delivered 
strong growth in recent periods, with its consolidated assets under management as at 30 September 2026 
approximately 38% higher than a year earlier, led by strong momentum in the gold loan business. This 
growth has been funded largely through internal accruals and borrowings which has resulted in a sharp 
increase in the Company’s leverage . As the Company’s loan book con Ɵnues to grow, the Company’s capital 
requirements necessitate a calibrated augmenta Ɵon of its equity capital to sustain growth within 
prudenƟal capital thresholds.  
  
The investment of ~INR 5,000 crore by the Investor 1 and ~INR 150 crore by the Anchorage Investors will 
contribute to the Tier 1 capital of the Company, strengthen its capital base and provide the Company with 
addiƟonal ﬁnancial ﬂexibility to pursue its business plans and growth opportuni Ɵes, while maintaining a 
comfortable buﬀer over regulatory capital requirements. The investment by the Investor 1 and Anchorage 
Investors is complemented by the proposed investment of up to ~INR 1,264 crore by the Promoters 
through the Subscrip Ɵon Warrants at the same price, which underscores the Promoters’ conﬁdence in the 
long-term prospects of the Company and their alignment with the Investor 1 and all other shareholders.  
 
Capital Adequacy and Risk Mi ƟgaƟon 
  
As a non-banking ﬁnancial company primarily engaged in lending against gold jewellery, the Company is 
required to maintain, on an ongoing basis, a minimum capital to risk- weighted assets ra Ɵo (“CRAR”) of 
15% and a minimum Tier 1 capital ra Ɵo of 12%. As at 30 September 2026, the Company’s CRAR and Tier 1 
capital raƟo stood at 17% and 12.5 % respecƟvely. 
  
In the absence of fresh capital, the Company would need to moderate the growth of its loan book to 
remain within the prescribed capital ra Ɵos, notwithstanding the strong demand for its products. A thin 
capital cushion also limits the Company’s ability to absorb unexpected credit losses and is a key 
considera Ɵon for the RBI, lenders and credit ra Ɵng agencies in their assessment of the Company.  
  
While the Company con Ɵnues to generate healthy internal accruals, these alone are not suﬃcient to 
support its growth plans while maintaining an adequate buﬀer over the regulatory minimum capital 
requirement. The Company therefore requires a signiﬁcant infusion of Tier 1 capital at this stage.  
 
Upon allotment of the Subscrip Ɵon Shares  and the Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares , and conversion of 
SubscripƟon Warrants , the Company’s Tier 1 capital will be strengthened by approximately INR 6,414 
crore. On a pro forma basis as at 30 September 2026, the infusion is expected to increase the Company’s 
CRAR from 17% to 30.3% and its Tier 1 capital ra Ɵo from 12.5% to 25.7%, providing a buﬀer of 
approximately 15.3 and 13.7 percentage points respec Ɵvely over the regulatory minimum, and to reduce 
its gearing from 4.6x to 2.8x, bringing the Company’s leverage to more conserva Ɵve levels. 
 
The investment by Blackstone, one of the world's leading alterna Ɵve asset managers, brings strong 
insƟtuƟonal backing to the Company, along with deep experience in ﬁnancial services and a long -term 
commitment to strengthening its governance, risk manag ement and opera Ɵng capabili Ɵes. A stronger 
capital posi Ɵon, lower leverage and the backing of a globally reputed ins ƟtuƟonal investor are expected 
to improve the Company’s risk proﬁle and o ver Ɵme, support a diversiﬁca Ɵon of funding sources , a 
reducƟon in cost of borrowings and an improvement in credit ra Ɵngs, which will in turn enhance 
proﬁtability and ability to serve customers compe ƟƟvely. A well -capitalised balance sheet will also 
enhance the Company’s resilience to external developments, including changes in the interest rate 
environment and evolving regulatory requirements. 
  
Why Compulsorily Conver Ɵble Preference Shares: The Preferred Structure  
  
The Board has evaluated various alterna Ɵves for raising capital and believes that a preferen Ɵal issue of 
compulsorily conver Ɵble preference shares to Investor 1 and Anchorage Investors is the most appropriate 
structure for the Company and its shareholders at this stage, for the reasons set out below. 
  
First, the Subscrip Ɵon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares provide the Company with immediate 
and permanent Tier 1 capital. The Subscrip Ɵon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares will be fully 
paid-up on allotment, and the en Ɵre subscrip Ɵon amount will be available to the Company upfront. The 
SubscripƟon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares are non-redeemable and the Company will have 
no obliga Ɵon to repay the subscrip Ɵon amount, which will become permanent equity capital on 
conversion.  
  
Second, the structure defers the dilu Ɵon of the equity vo Ɵng rights of exis Ɵng shareholders. Each 
SubscripƟon Share and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Share will mandatorily convert into 1 (one) Equity Share 
at the earlier of (i) such date as may be mutually agreed between the holders of the compulsorily 
converƟble preference shares and the Company  or (ii) mandatorily, on the expiry of 17 (seventeen) months 
and 15 (ﬁ Ōeen) days from the date of allotment. Un Ɵl conversion, the Subscrip Ɵon Shares and Anchorage 
SubscripƟon Shares will carry only such vo Ɵng rights as are available to holders of pre ference shares under 
applicable law. The Company can therefore deploy the capital in its business immediately, while the 
diluƟon of the equity vo Ɵng rights of all other shareholders is deferred un Ɵl conversion.  
 
Third, the Subscrip Ɵon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares oﬀer certainty to shareholders. The 
SubscripƟon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares will convert into Equity Shares at an eﬀec Ɵve price 
of INR 602 (Indian Rupees Six Hundred and Two only) per Equity Share, which is above the ﬂoor price 
determined in accordance with Chapter V of the SEBI ICDR Regula Ɵons with reference to the Relevant 
Date. The conversion ra Ɵo is ﬁxed at the Ɵme of issue and is subject only to customary adjustments for 
capital restructuring; there is no mechanism for the conversion price to be modiﬁed. Shareholders 
therefore have full visibility today of the maximum number of Equity Shares to be issued on conversion, 
being 8,30,56,478 Equity Shares to Investor 1, 9,96,678 Equity Shares to Investor 2 and 14,95,017 Equity 
Shares to Investor 3 . A preferen Ɵal issue to long -term investor(s) also provides certainty on the quantum, 
pricing and Ɵming of the capital raise, which is par Ɵcularly valuable in the context of recent vola Ɵlity in 
equity markets and in the NBFC sector, as compared with market-dependent fund-raising routes. 
  
Fourth, the dividend terms of the Subscrip Ɵon Shares and Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares are aligned with 
value crea Ɵon for all shareholders. The Company will not be obligated to pay any preferen Ɵal dividend to 
Investor 1 and Anchorage Investors unless the share price of the Company is below INR 825 per Equity 
Share at the Ɵme of conversion. The preferen Ɵal dividend reduces and falls away en Ɵrely as value is 
created for all shareholders. The dividend linearly increases from INR 0 per Subscrip Ɵon Share/  Anchorage 
SubscripƟon Share at INR 825 share price to a maximum of INR 108 (implying a cap of ~12% p.a.) as 
dividend per Subscrip Ɵon Share/ Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Share at or below INR 717 per share price on the 
Mandatory Conversion Date. The Subscrip Ɵon Shares/ Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares will have the right 
to parƟcipate in any dividend declared on the Equity Shares on an as -if-converted basis and such dividend 
shall be reduced from the above- menƟoned preferen Ɵal dividend, if any.  
  
Finally, compulsorily conver Ɵble preference shares are a well -established instrument for investment by 
foreign investors in Indian companies and qualify as equity instruments under the Foreign Exchange 
Management (Non-debt Instruments) Rules, 2019. The SubscripƟon Shares will be fully rupee-
denominated, and neither the Company nor its exis Ɵng shareholders will bear any foreign exchange 
exposure in rela Ɵon to the Preferen Ɵal Issue. 
 
Why Warrants for the Promoters: Aligned and Phased Par ƟcipaƟon 
 
The Promoters are commiƫng substan Ɵal (INR 1,264 Crores, ~4x the rights subscribed to in 2024)  fresh 
capital at the same price as Investor 1 and Anchorage Investors, which aligns their interests with those of 
all the shareholders, ensures addi Ɵonal skin in the game, and reﬂects their conﬁdence in the Company’s 
long-term prospects. The Promoters' par ƟcipaƟon through the Subscrip Ɵon Warrants mi Ɵgates the 
diluƟon of the aggregate shareholding of the promoter and promoter group.  The Subscrip Ɵon Warrants 
are priced at INR 602 (Indian Rupees Six Hundred and Two only) per Subscrip Ɵon Warrant (priced above 
the regulatory pricing guidelines) each carrying the right to subscribe to 1 (one) Equity Share, the same as 
the Subscrip Ɵon Shares and the Anchorage Subscrip Ɵon Shares, and carry no vo Ɵng or dividend rights 
unƟl exercised. ~INR 316 crore (25% of the considera Ɵon) is payable on allotment and is forfeited if the 
warrants are not exercised. The balance of ~INR 948 crore is payable on exercise within 18 (eighteen) 
months, adding further equity capital as the Company grows while phasing the related dilu Ɵon. Consistent 
with their track record of suppor Ɵng the Company's prior capital raises, including full par ƟcipaƟon in the 
Company's rights issue in 2024, the Promoters are par ƟcipaƟng in the Preferen Ɵal Issue through the 
SubscripƟon Warrants.  
 
Conclusion: A Con Ɵnued Long -term Growth Trajectory  
 
The Board believes that the Preferen Ɵal Issue represents a signiﬁcant milestone in the Company’s growth 
journey. It strengthens the Company’s capital adequacy upfront, ins ƟtuƟonalizes long -term stability in the 
shareholder base through the entry of a global, credible investor, and aﬃrms conﬁdence in the Company’s 
strategic direc Ɵon, for the beneﬁt of all its stakeholders.

Investor 1 
 
Why Blackstone: The Ideal Partner  
 
Investor 1, i.e. Ingot SG Pte. Ltd., incorporated under the laws of Singapore, having its registered oﬃce at 
77 Robinson Road, #13- 00, Robinson 77, Singapore 068896, is an aﬃliate of, or a fund ul Ɵmately managed 
by, Blackstone Inc. (NYSE:BX) (" Blackstone "). Blackstone is the world’s largest alterna Ɵve asset manager, 
with Assets under Management (AUM) of nearly USD 1.4 trillion as at 30 September 2026, across private 
equity, real estate, credit, infrastructure, life sciences, growth equity, secondaries and hedge fund 
strategies. India is one of the largest markets for Blackstone globally and it recently completed 20 years in 
India and has built leading businesses in private equity and real estate in the country. Blackstone has a 
strong public market track record having been promoters in 11 listed companies in India and investors in 
20+ listed companies in India. Blackstone has demonstr ated a track record across its 260+ global por ƞolio 
companies of partnering with management teams to drive sustainable value crea Ɵon and opera Ɵonal 
improvement across diverse sectors and jurisdic Ɵons, as well as consistent improvement in ins ƟtuƟonal 
conﬁdence following its investments. 
 
Blackstone has a strong track record in ﬁnancial services with investments in India as well as globally. 
Blackstone is the promoter of Aadhar Housing Finance Limited (one of India’s leading aﬀordable housing 
ﬁnance companies, acquired in 2019, publicly listed in 2024) and is also a strategic investor in The Federal 
Bank Limited (one of India’s leading private sector banks, acquired in 2025). This experience of partnering 
with regulated NBFCs / lenders in India, including in strengthening their governance, risk management and 
funding proﬁles, is directly relevant to the Company’s next phase of growth. 
 
While Investor 1 will be a minority shareholder with this transac Ɵon, the Company will have the 
opportunity to tap into Blackstone’s network of its 50+ investee companies and their 2.2 lakh employees 
in India within their group on an arm’s length basis. In addi Ɵon, this support enhances the Company’s 
corporate credibility with ra Ɵng agencies, regulators, and ins ƟtuƟonal investors. This represents convic Ɵon 
capital— signalling to the market not only that our strategy is eﬀec Ɵve, but that it merits sustained, long -
term support and conﬁdence. 
 
Anchorage Investors 
 
Anchorage Capital Scheme II, a scheme of category II alterna Ɵve investment fund registered with the 
SecuriƟes and Exchange Board of India,  and Anchorage Capital Scheme III, a scheme of category II 
alternaƟve investment fund registered with SEBI,  are schemes of Anchorage Capital, a SEBI-registered 
Category II Alterna Ɵve Investment Fund (AIF). Anchorage Capital is an India -centric investment fund 
managing over US$1 billion of assets under management (AUM). The fund is led by Mr. Rohit Kothari, who 
brings more than 25 years of investment experience and a strong track record of iden Ɵfying and suppor Ɵng 
high-growth businesses. Anchorage Capital has invested across diverse sectors and has backed companies 
such as Meril Life Sciences, India's largest home-grown medical device manufacturer; Neysa Networks, 
one of India's leading AI neocloud pla ƞorms; Nxtra Data, India's largest data centre network; Qi Cap, an 
AI-driven quan ƟtaƟve trading pla ƞorm; Precision Wires, one of Asia's leading winding wire manufacturers; 
Thriveni Earthmovers, one of India's largest private mineral value-chain enterprises; and Enviro Infra 
Engineers, a leading water and wastewater treatment EPC company. The Anchorage Investors will not have 
any special rights in the Company.

 ANNEXURE B: DRAFT OF THE AMENDED AND RESTATED ARTICLES OF ASSOCIATION OF THE COMPANY 
 
Note: *The Name changed from ‘IIFL Holdings Limited’ to ‘IIFL Finance Limited’ pursuant to 
the Composite Scheme of Arrangement approved by the Shareholders at their meeting held 
on December 12, 2018 and approved by Hon'ble National Company Law Tribunal, Mumbai 
Bench vide its Order dated March 07, 2019 .  
 
THE COMPANIES ACT, 2013 
 
COMPANY LIMITED BY SHARES 
 
ARTICLE OF ASSOCIATION 
OF 
*IIFL FINANCE LIMITED 
 
This set of Ar Ɵcles has been approved pursuant to the provisions of Sec Ɵon 14 of the Companies 
Act, 2013 and by a special resolu Ɵon passed at the Extraordinary General Mee Ɵng of IIFL Finance 
Limited (“ Company ”) held on 4 December 2026. These Ar Ɵcles have been adopted as the Ar Ɵcles 
of Associa Ɵon of the Company in subs ƟtuƟon for and to the exclusion of all the exis Ɵng ArƟcles 
thereof. 
 
The arƟcles of associa Ɵon of the Company comprise of 2 (two) parts, Part A and Part B, which parts 
shall, unless the context otherwise requires, co-exist with each other. In the case of inconsistency 
or contradic Ɵons, conﬂict or overlap between Part A and Part B, the provisions of Part B shall prevail 
and be applicable. All cross references to an Ar Ɵcle or ArƟcles or any Schedule in this Part B shall be 
references to an Ar Ɵcle or ArƟcles or Schedules of Part B of these Ar Ɵcles. For the avoidance of  
doubt, it is clariﬁed that the provisions of Part B of these Ar Ɵcles shall be applicable to, and bind, all 
members of the Company and the Company itself. 
 
PART A 
 
1. The regulations contained in Table "F" in the First Schedule to the 
Companies Act, 2013 shall not apply to this Company, but the regulations 
for the management of the Company and for the observance of the 
Members thereof and their representatives shall, subject to any exercise of 
the statutory powers of the Company with reference to the repeal or 
alteration of, or addition to, its Articles by Special Resolution, as prescribed 
by the said Companies Act, 2013, be such as are contained in these Articles, 
unless the same are repugnant or contrary to the provisions of Companies 
Act, 2013, rules made there under or any amendment or notification 
thereto.

   CAPITAL AND INCREASE AND REDUCTION OF CAPITAL  
 
6. (1) The Authorised Share Capital of the Company shall be as specified  
in Clause V of the Memorandum of Association of the Company. Division of Capital  
 (2) The Company shall be entitled to issue, offer and allot fresh and further 
shares, Debentures and other Securities in dematerialized form 
pursuant to and in accordance with the provisions under the 
Depositories Act and it shall also be entitled to dematerialise its existing 
shares, Debentures and other Securities, subject to the provisions of the 
Act. In this connection, the Company shall comply with all the applicable 
provisions of the Depositories Act.  
7. Subject to the provisions of the Act and these Articles the shares in the 
capital of the Company for the time being (including any shares forming 
part of any increased capital of the Company) shall be under the control of 
the Board who may allot or otherwise dispose of the same or any of them 
to such persons, in such proportion and on such terms and conditions and 
either at a premium or at par or (subject to compliance with the provisions 
of Section 54 of the Act) at a discount or as may be prescribed under the 
act and at such times as it may from time to time think fit and proper and, 
with the consent of the general meeting, give to any person the option to 
call for or be allotted any class of shares of the Company either at par or at 
a premium or, subject as aforesaid at a discount, such option being 
exercisable at such times and for such consideration as the Board thinks fit. Share under the 
control of the 
Board 
 
8. In addition to and without derogating from the powers for that purpose 
conferred on the Board under Article 7, the Company in general meeting 
may determine that any share (whether forming part of the original capital 
or of any increased capital of the Company) shall be offered to such persons 
(whether Members or not) in such proportions and on such terms and 
conditions and either at a premium or at par or (subject to compliance with 
the provisions of the Section 53 of the Act) at a discount, as such general 
meeting shall determine and with full power to give any person (whether a 
Member or not) the option to call for or be allotted any class of shares of 
the Company either at a premium or at par, or (subject to compliance with 
the provisions of Section 54 of the Act) at a discount, such option being 
exercisable at such times and for such consideration as may be directed by 
such general meeting or the Company in general meeting may make any 
other provisions, whatsoever for the issue, allotment or disposal of any 
shares. Power of 
Company in 
General Meeting 
to issue Shares 
9. The Company by a Resolution in general meeting may from time to time, 
increase its share capital by the creation of further shares, such increases 
to be of such aggregate amount and to be divided into shares of such 
respective amounts as the resolution shall prescribe. Subject to the 
provisions of the Act, the further shares shall be issued upon such terms 
and conditions and with such rights and privileges annexed thereto, as the 
general meeting resolving upon the creation thereof shall direct, and if no 
direction be given as Board shall determine; and in particular, such shares 
may be issued with a preferential or qualified or differential right to 
dividends and in distribution of assets of the Company, and with a right of 
voting at general meetings of the Company. Increase in capital 
 10. 1. Where at any time, it is proposed to increase the subscribed capital of 
the Company by issue of further shares, then, such further shares shall 
be offered to- Further issue of 
capital 
(a) the persons who, at the date of the offer, are holders of the  
equity shares of the Company, in proportion, as nearly as 
circumstances admit, to the capital paid-up in those shares at that 
date; 
(b) the offer aforesaid shall be made by notice dispatched through 
registered post or speed post or through electronic mode or such 
other means as may be permitted, to all the existing shareholders at 
least three days before the opening of the issue, specifying the 
number of shares offered and limiting a time, not being less than 
fifteen days but not exceeding thirty days from the date of the offer, 
within which the offer if not accepted, will be deemed to have 
declined; 
(c) the offer aforesaid shall be deemed to include a right exercisable by 
the person concerned to renounce the shares offered to them in 
favour of any other person and the Notice referred to in sub clause 
(b) hereof shall contain a statement of this right. 
PROVIDED THAT the Directors may decline, without assigning any 
reason to allot any shares to any person in whose favour any Member 
may renounce the shares offered to him. 
(d) after the expiry of the time specified in the notice aforesaid, or on the 
receipt of earlier intimation from the person to whom such notice is 
given that he declines to accept the shares offered, the Board of 
Directors may dispose of them in such manner as they think most 
beneficial to the Company and is not dis-advantageous to the 
shareholders. Notwithstanding anything contained in clause (a) of this 
articles, the further shares aforesaid may be offered in any manner 
whatsoever and to any person or persons, whether or not such person 
or persons include persons who, at the date of the offer, are holders 
of the equity shares of the Company, if such offer is authorised by the 
Special Resolution of the Company in general meeting or where no 
such Special Resolution is passed, if the votes cast (whether on a show 
of hands or on a poll as the case may be) in favour of the proposal 
contained in the resolution moved in the general meeting (including 
the casting vote, if any, of the Chairman) by the Members who, being 
entitled to do so, vote in person, or where proxies are allowed, by 
proxy, exceed the votes, if any, cast against the proposal by Members, 
so entitled and voting and the Central Government is satisfied, on an 
application made by the Board of Directors in this behalf that the 
proposal is most beneficial to the Company. 
(e) employees under a scheme of employees’ stock option, subject to 
Special Resolution passed by Company in general meeting and subject 
to such conditions as may be prescribed; 
 (f) to any persons, if it is authorised by a Special Resolution, whether or 
not those persons include the persons referred to in clause (a), clause 
(b), clause (c), clause (d) either for cash or for a consideration other 
than cash, if the price of such shares is determined by the valuation 
report of a registered valuer subject to such conditions as may be 
prescribed. 
    
 2. Nothing in clause (d) of Article 10 hereof shall be deemed; 
(i) To extend the time within which the offer should be accepted; 
or 
(ii) To authorise any person to exercise the right of renunciation

 3. Nothing in this Article shall apply to the increase of the subscribed
